# Tasks (the live list)

**Workspace ID:** SPARMVET_VIZ  
**Last sorted:** 2026-11-13. Only open and current tasks are here. Done tasks are in `.claude/tasks/tasks_archive_<unit>.md` (rule: `rules_verification_testing.md` section 4); superseded tasks, session notes and the old completed-phases table are in `outdated/tasks_before_sort_2026-11-13.md`; the original text of the backlog tasks that were shortened or merged on 2026-11-13 is in `outdated/tasks_backlog_details_2026-11-13.md`. The map of the whole `.claude` folder is `.claude/INDEX.md`.

**State of 2026-11-26 for the HOME audit and the lineage (read before the sections below):** `.claude/design/home_audit_what_is_true_now.md` is the page to trust. A task below that speaks of "This plot only / All plots / All plots except", of tick lines or default ticks of the audit dialog, of a propagation dialog, of per-plot stacks or of "My view" is OLD THINKING: the scope of a step is the place chosen in "Path of the data" (D-0662). The removal is the task AUDIT-OLD-SCOPE-REMOVE-1 (open since D-0672: the hold is released, the separate Apply goes); the slices are HOME-SLICE-1, 2, 3A, 3B, Q1, 4, Q2, Q3 and S-BAR (section 1, before DECISION-HASH-REMOVE-1), in the order of the plan `tmpAI/status/DEMO_BUILD_PLAN_2026-11-26.md` (its LATEST block): 3a, 3b, FLOW-1 (= Q2 with the first part of 4), FLOW-2 (= the rest of 4 with Q3), FLOW-3 (= AUDIT-OLD-SCOPE-REMOVE-1, with the one rename), S-bar. The audit log is AUDIT-LOG-DESIGN-AND-BUILD. Two tasks that the place model changes (AUDIT-DEFAULT-TICKS-REVIEW, AUDIT-DIALOG-TABLE-NO-PLOT-READS) stay in this list, on hold until the owner confirms (D-0649).

## How to read this file

| Group | What it is | Waits for |
|---|---|---|
| 1. HOME audit build | the redesign of HOME (Exploration and Audit); packages P0-P12 of the stage-2 plan | Eve's reading of `spaces/HOME.md` and the stage-2 spec, then P0 |
| 2. Recipe format | the recipe format, checker and migration (the recipe session, issue #73) | the recipe session; the HOME build depends on part of it |
| 3. Configuration stage | names, gating, deployment resources | last, after the build |
| 4. Backlog | older tasks, not triaged with Eve | Eve's triage |

## Rules for this file

- A task is one line (or one short block) with its id, `[model/effort]` tag and, when it has one, its issue number; context goes in the design files, not here.
- Done: move the task to a file in `archives/` and add a pointer at the bottom. Superseded: move it to `outdated/` with the reason. No `Completed` notes inside this file.
- Session notes and decisions go in the design files, the guide's decision register or the ADR log, not here.
- New tasks carry a model and effort tag (`CLAUDE.md` section 2).
- Eve 2026-11-15: builds on a recommendation are PROVISIONAL (recorded with the commit, reversible) when no result changes silently and nothing is hidden; open points go to her in ONE ordered list.

- [ ] **INT-TYPE-1** `[sonnet/medium]` — Eve decided 2026-11-13 (O24): add an `integer` type for `input_fields` and `cast` (counts, years: no more `2022.0`). Mine: ingestion map (`ingestor.py:169`, `metadata_validator.py:23`), `cast` options, tests first (year, count, a value with a decimal, a missing value); the `QA_L50_integer` canary then needs no explicit cast. Recipe session: the checker enum, the spec, the docs. **Amended 2026-11-13 (Eve asked: why not follow Polars?):** one vocabulary for the type of a column in `input_fields` and in `cast`: the Polars names (`Int64`, `Float64`, `String`, `Boolean`, `Date`, `Categorical`); it removes the `categorical` ambiguity (String at ingestion, Categorical in the validator; evidence file section 11). Polars strict behaviour: a file with `2022.0` in an `Int64` column fails and names the value (no special whole-number rule). `cast` Float64 to Int64 must FAIL when a value is not whole (today it truncates `2022.5` to `2022` silently: a science risk). Decided (Eve 2026-11-13, sixth round, DEC27): `categorical` is refused ("this was String"); every `categorical` in the shipped recipes becomes `String` (run A); `Categorical` stays available as a Polars type.

## 5. Eve's decisions of 2026-11-15 (recorded by the coordinator, 2026-11-15 09:42; channel M87 to M90 from sparmvet-viz-57)

Each line is one decision, dated; the task it belongs to is named.
- **[x] JOIN-KEY-ONE-1** `[sonnet/medium]` (done, merge `5598a7fd`, 2026-11-15) — DECIDED 2026-11-15 (D7, option b, channel M87): "definitively; three codes that do the same thing is a problem; one source of truth." One pure join-key rule in `libs/utils`, called by the build (`DataAssembler._check_join_key_types`), the Blueprint preview (`check_join_keys`) and the checker (`join_key_problems`). No behaviour change; the parity tests stay as proof that the three callers agree; then the duplicated code is removed. General principle (Eve): wherever the same rule exists in several copies, one source of truth. Starts after `row-order` is merged.
- **[ ] SELF-JOIN-1** — DECIDED 2026-11-15 (D8, option a, channel M88): never skip a join step silently; every join runs as written; a join of a table with itself gets a clear WARNING, not an error. Eve's use case: joining two tables on themselves to reorganise data (calibration versus real measurements before splitting). Warnings are visibly distinct from errors. OPEN: a data-scientist viewpoint says whether any self-join case should be an ERROR; no error is built before Eve's answer.
- **[x] BP-PREVIEW-ERRORS-1** `[sonnet/low]` (D13 done, merge `839ffac3`, 2026-11-15) — DECIDED 2026-11-15 (D13, channel M89), early: the Blueprint join preview catches only the expected errors; a programming error is not shown as the user's mistake.
- **[x] D10 (template key)** (done, merge `839ffac3`, 2026-11-15) — DECIDED 2026-11-15 (channel M89): in recipes a sample id must be a String or Categorical (a recipe problem, not an app fault). Change the template key AND add the checker advice against a float join key (the advice exists: J13). Always add a checker advice when it helps.
- **[ ] OLD-WORDS-1** `[sonnet/medium]` (D14: old uses in two files done, merge `839ffac3`; six design files that say "project overview" become "recipe overview" (Eve N13 (a)); the exclusions wait for the list of N12, see OLD-WORDS-LIST-1 in section 6) — DECIDED 2026-11-15 (D14, channel M89): ALL old words corrected everywhere in every ACTIVE file and in the docs; NOT in history, deprecated or tombstone content. A scout lists the hits per word first; where a word is current vocabulary (for example the checker's `profile`, the project root) the exclusion is proposed for Eve to see.
- **[ ] D15 (hypothesis)** — DECIDED 2026-11-15 (channel M89): wait until the stage-2 spec is confirmed, then do it (the audit step, the T3 key and the per-collection key tests); decide `hypothesis` then.

The fourth point changes: length alone is not the signal. Many DIVERSE steps at different places (other kinds, tables or plots) are the warning, because they may be an attempt to clean the data until the result looks right. A series of the same kind counts as one change. **Raw-YAML step and the two recipes**
- [ ] **TWO-RECIPES, original recipe and audit recipe** `[sonnet/high]` — DECIDED 2026-11-15 (M125; it corrects line 3 of M124). Five lines. (1) The original recipe is never edited while auditing; it stays the reference. (2) The audit recipe is a copy. Its original steps cannot be edited or removed in the audit. (3) The audit only adds steps. A step added in the audit can be removed again; that removes it from the audit recipe only, and the original recipe is not touched. (4) The export is the audit recipe: the original steps and the audit steps still present. (5) Full recipe editing exists only in Blueprint. The two names, "original recipe" and "audit recipe", are used the same way everywhere. The check: the build, the spec (the copy minus its marked steps equals the original; only marked steps can be removed) and the wording, against these lines. State: decided; the check waits for UML-CHECK.

Stands: Data Import writes only into a writable data place and never into a read-only place. DECIDED 2026-11-21, D-0434: an upload with the name of an existing data file replaces it, in the user's writable place only, after a plain confirmation; no copy, no archive, no new name. Her words: "not out business to decide what the user needs to analyze"; "writes are done where a user can write". Her view: for recipes the dated name means an old file is never overwritten, so no archive is needed, except a same-day correction through BLUEPRINT; no decided rule covers data file names. The two PROVISIONAL refusals built on 2026-11-21 (a `by_dataset_id` deployment; a recipe imported from a bundle) stay. Not built. 2026-11-21, D-0438: confirmed by her: a data file whose name exists is replaced after a confirmation (D-0434 stands). Facts: today an upload is written over the file the recipe reads (`app/modules/orchestrator.py:197-209`), with no copy and no record of the uploaded name or date; the upload control is shown when `testing_mode` is true, and it is true when the key is absent (`app/src/bootloader.py:903-905`; true in `developer`, `qa`, `standalone`, `pipeline-exploration-advanced`); a saved session restored on other data only gives a warning for 8 s and opens (`app/handlers/session_handlers.py:607-616`), so the confirmation example 'N saved sessions made with it will no longer open' is not what the code does. Both viewpoints: an upload goes only to the user's own data place; one step 'choose the data for this table' with two sources (a browser upload that is copied; a file of a declared place that is only pointed to); for data already on the server the app lists the files of the declared places and the user picks one. Blocking fact for D-0426: the load gate rejects at start a recipe whose data is absent (`app/src/bootloader.py:431-447`), so it never reaches the selector. 2026-11-21: her remark that picking data from a declared place "was already in place in home" was checked in the code and in the git history (`tmpAI/coordinator/home_data_picker/GIT_FACTS.md`, `SCOUT_FACTS.md`): a control that lets a user pick a DATA file or folder was never built; the planned 'active data selector' step has no commit; what exists since April 2026 is the recipe selector (one declared recipes folder). Picking data from a declared place is new work, with the recipe selector as its model.
  2026-11-21, D-0439: how data reaches the app is defined by the configuration of the deployment. Her likely cases: a static deployment (no upload); a deployment where the user has a dedicated read-write directory and another way of transferring data. The review session's reading (to confirm): the base is that the app reads from declared places and the user picks a file from a list; the browser upload is an option the deployer switches on, written into one named read_write place. Open: the upload off unless declared; how the list looks and which places it lists.

The known fault stays documented: a column picked right after a REAL change of columns, while the plots are drawn, is lost (15 of 15 in the builder's scripted probe). Evidence: `tmpAI/coordinator/filter_column_reset_report.md` and `tmpAI/coordinator/filter_column_reset_REVIEW.md`. Probe: 15 of 15 tries (switch plot tab, choose `year` 0 to 300 ms later). Reasoned cause: the server cannot see a click that waits behind its running work. Options as first recorded: A accept it and document it (the form is rebuilt after a plot change; choose the column after the plot has loaded); B make the app less busy on a plot change (a performance task; it narrows the window, it does not remove it); C a notice (the server would have to guess what was clicked); D a small client-side piece that holds the last pick across the rebuild (removes the loss; excluded by the present rule unless the owner allows it). The builder recommends A, with B as a task, and D only if the owner accepts a client-side piece (reasoned). Sources: `tmpAI/coordinator/filter_column_reset_report.md` (point 1 and the choice), `tmpAI/coordinator/filter_column_reset_REVIEW.md` (point 7); channel M327 of 78 (coordinator). - [ ] **DATA-FILE-CUT-ACCEPTED** `[sonnet/high]` (found 2026-11-21 by a run for the study of deployments with no user; a result changes silently). HOME, RUN on a scratch static page: a data file cut in the middle of a line is accepted with no message and the plot is drawn from 21 of 42 samples (`tmpAI/panel/deployment_no_user/RUN_FACTS.md`, case 7a). A file cut inside a multi-byte character is refused. CAUSE FOUND 2026-11-21, report for the owner `tmpAI/coordinator/data_file_cut/REPORT.md` (all cases run headless on a copy of a demo file): the app reads a tab-separated file with the reading library's defaults (`libs/ingestion/src/ingestion/ingestor.py:141, 200, 211`: plain `scan_csv`); the library fills a row that has fewer fields than the header with empty values and says nothing; no argument of the library makes it an error, so the app would have to count the fields itself. Accepted today by the reader, by HOME's Data Import check and by the load gate (it reads one row, `app/modules/data_analysis_recipe_validator.py:535`): a file cut in the middle of a line, cut exactly at the end of a line, or cut inside the last value (a wrong last value is then read). Refused: a cut inside a quoted field, invalid UTF-8. Also found: a row with MORE fields than the header is refused only when the extra field falls in a column the recipe reads (the app keeps only declared columns, `ingestor.py:65`), so the text of `ui_implementation_contract.md` section 9 point 2 claims more than is true. Decided: nothing covers a row with fewer fields than the header (her decisions cover the header's columns, the value types, and 'no silent error'). A cut exactly at the end of a line cannot be detected from the file alone: it needs a row count or a hash delivered with the file. The behaviour is the library's default since 2026-04-07 and was never decided. NO FIX before her choice among the five options of the report (the reviewer's suggestion: refuse a row where a declared field is missing and name the row; make the 'more fields' check real; decide separately on a delivered count or hash for pipeline deployments).

Her three cases: (a) one recipe shows data refreshed every week and pushed into a directory; (b) a pipeline that needs a tmp location the app can read and write and the user cannot; (c) a static deployment with several data files and where each is fetched. Questions to answer (the review session's list, not her words): how this is stated in the deployment specification without any link to a user (an app-owned working place: the stored tables' place or another named place; how it differs from the user place; its access word); what the app writes there and never writes; what happens when it is not writable (choice 5 of the deployment study, still open); what "fetched" means (where fetched files land, how the app knows they changed, the hash that keeps a figure reproducible); the cases she did not name (several refreshed folders; a failed or partial refresh while the app runs; two copies of the app on one folder; a refresh that changes the columns). Part of the one deployment specification (D-0417, D-0418). Where it is written: per her rule D-0419 and D-0421 this study is written in `.claude/design/studies/deployment_no_user/` from its first day IF she has confirmed the subfolder naming by then; until then its files are under `tmpAI/panel/deployment_no_user/` and are listed for the move. Step 1b DONE 2026-11-21: `tmpAI/panel/deployment_no_user/RUN_FACTS.md` (ten cases run on a scratch static page). Step 2 DONE 2026-11-21: four viewpoints (`architect.md`, `scientist.md`, `developer.md`, `deployer_and_visitor.md` in `tmpAI/panel/deployment_no_user/`). Step 3 DONE 2026-11-21: the table for the owner `tmpAI/panel/deployment_no_user/TABLE_FOR_THE_OWNER.md` (484 lines; it starts with what changes a result silently; a proposed configuration text for her three cases and the form of a list of delivered files; 19 choices, ranked). All four viewpoints say: a static page writes no session record; fetching is done outside the app. Not settled between them: one place or two for the app; a list of delivered files required or optional; on a failed delivery keep the last good data or stop; data absent at start: the process exits or a plain page. Waits for her reading; nothing is built. Same table, found on the way (no design decision needed, not fixed): the export notice says 'install Quarto' when the temporary folder is the cause (`app/handlers/export_handlers.py:2070-2076`); the refusal text blames the recipe when a data file changed (`libs/ingestion/src/ingestion/ingestor.py:50-51`); a start-time message names things that do not exist (`app/src/bootloader.py:143-144`); the documents say the `cache` place is written (`docs/workflows/connector.qmd:75, 131, 137`, `config/deployment/templates/deployment_template.yaml:65-67`); a user folder that cannot be written gives a blank grey page (`app/src/server.py:149`).
  2026-11-21, D-0429: choice 6 answered: no `users:` word; what the app saves for a person is in a user place given by system variables, one copy of the app per person; a shared place where people save exists only if the administrator declares it; a static deployment on Connect has no user place and no save control. Open: the deployer who shares one copy with a writable user place among several people. Step 2: viewpoints (architect, scientist, developer, a deployer who is not a developer). Step 3: one table for her plus the proposed configuration text.
  2026-11-21, D-0442: added to this study by her: how new data and a new recipe reach a static page on a web server such as Posit Connect; she thinks Connect redeploys the app when data or recipes change: NOT verified; being checked against the platforms' own documentation (DONE 2026-11-21: `tmpAI/panel/deployment_no_user/PLATFORMS_REFRESH.md`, each statement quoted from the platform's own documentation. Answer: NO, Posit Connect does not redeploy or restart an app by itself when data or recipe files change on disk; the one automatic way is git-backed content (a fetch about every 15 minutes, a redeploy only when the watched branch and folder changed); a deploy makes a new application folder and files written to the working folder are removed at each deploy; scheduling applies to reports only, interactive apps cannot be scheduled; other ways: a redeploy by an outside job, a manual redeploy, files on the server or a mounted folder read while the app runs, pins. The app already reads the files again at each opening, so new data needs no restart; the weak points are an open page that keeps the old picture, a half-delivered file, and a refusal that blames the recipe. Five options for a static page, none chosen. Not verified: Connect's default process settings, a shared path on a plain Connect, the Galaxy refresh model, Connect Cloud and shinyapps.io.).
